				\begin{description}
					\item[Online Access] \href{https://www.josephsmithpapers.org/paper-summary/discourse-circa-early-march-1843/1}{Here}
					% \item[Analysis] \hyperref[XX]{Here}
				\end{description}
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Background]
				\end{description}
				
					% It might also be useful to give a two sentence context of the period: e.g., "This speech was given in Nauvoo to a small congregation one month prior to the destruction of the Nauvoo Expositor. These and other tensions may have been on the prophet's mind when he gave the speech."
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Original Source]
				\end{description}
				
					INTERIM CONTENT
				
					% brief description of original source material, with reference to JSP or somewhere else for more info
					% Background material: it might be helpful to have a note that says whether a given document was presented as a speech, was written in a journal, etc.
					% Also a comment here about how reliable the source might be, or what shape it is in, if there are large omissions or parts that are hard to understand, and so on.
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Text]
				\end{description}
				
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-CIR-EAR-MAR-1843-TS-a}%
					He
						\marginnote{a}%
					expressed his gratitude to elder [Heber C.] Kimball in the strongest terms, for having commenced and carried on in so masterly a manner the good and glorious work he had undertaken. He said it would be the means of doing a great deal of good, and of benefitting his young friends more than they were aware of:
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-CIR-EAR-MAR-1843-TS-b}%
					that
						\marginnote{b}%
					the gratitude of all good men, and of the young people whom he had so much benefitted, would follow him through life; and ``when gray hairs should his temples adorn,'' he could look back with pleasure upon the winter of 1843, when he was engaged in promoting the cause of benevolence, and prepairing his young friends for the glorious career which awaited them.
					
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-CIR-EAR-MAR-1843-TS-c}%
					He
						\marginnote{c}%
					said that he stood before them with more embarrassment, than he would before kings, nobles, and great men of the earth, for he knew the crimes of which they were guilty, and knew precisely how to address them; but his young friends before whom he now stood were guilty of none of these crimes, and he hardly knew what to say. He said he had never in his life seen such a large company of young people assembled together, pay such strict attention, listen with such profound silence, and keep such good order, as the assembly now before him. He praised their good conduct, and taught them how to behave in all places, explained to them their duty, and advised them to organize themselves into a society for the relief of the poor.
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-CIR-EAR-MAR-1843-TS-d}%
					As
						\marginnote{d}%
					a commencement to their benevolent efforts, he offered a petitition from an English brother by the name of Modesley [Sutcliffe Maudsley], who was lame, and who wished them to build him a house, that he might have a home among the saints: he had gathered together a few materials for this purpose, but was unable to use them; and, now, relying upon the active benevolence of the young people of Nauvoo, he sends in this petition that you may act upon it as you deem proper.
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-CIR-EAR-MAR-1843-TS-e}%
					He
						\marginnote{e}%
					advised them to choose a committee to collect funds for this purpose, and perform this charitable work as soon as the weather became suitable.--- He gave them much good advice, to guide their conduct through life and prepare them for a glorious eternity. He said he was very much pleased with the course elder Kimball had taken, and hoped he would continue his meetings and that the young people would follow his teachings.
					
		% -----
		\subsubsection{Letter to James Arlington Bennet, 17 March 1843}
		% -----
			\label{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843}
			% \label{EMS-1843-JS-DAY-3LETTERMONTH-YEAR}
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Print Sources]
			\end{description}

				\begin{tabular}{p{0.5in}p{1in}p{0.5in}p{1in}}
				JSP	 	& ---		& JSR 		& --- \\
				DC	 	& ---		& HC 		& --- \\
				HDDC 	& ---		& TCHC 		& --- \\
				\end{tabular}
				% HDDC = woodford's DC diss; JSR = Marquardt's JS Revelations; TCHC = Vogel HC
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Online Access] \href{https://www.josephsmithpapers.org/paper-summary/letter-to-james-arlington-bennet-17-march-1843/1}{Here}
				% \item[Analysis] \hyperref[XX]{Here}
			\end{description}
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Background]
			\end{description}
			
				% It might also be useful to give a two sentence context of the period: e.g., "This speech was given in Nauvoo to a small congregation one month prior to the destruction of the Nauvoo Expositor. These and other tensions may have been on the prophet's mind when he gave the speech."
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Original Source]
			\end{description}
			
				INTERIM CONTENT
			
				% brief description of original source material, with reference to JSP or somewhere else for more info
				% Background material: it might be helpful to have a note that says whether a given document was presented as a speech, was written in a journal, etc.
				% Also a comment here about how reliable the source might be, or what shape it is in, if there are large omissions or parts that are hard to understand, and so on.
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Text]
			\end{description}
			
				\begin{tightright}
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-a}%
					City
						\marginnote{a}%
					of Nauvoo, Ill. March 17, 1843.
				\end{tightright}

				Maj. Gen. [James Arlington] Bennet

				Dear Sir,--- I was not a little gratified at the reception of your favor of the 20\supers{th.} ult. It is true--- I am free from the ``unrighteous persecution'' got up by [John C.] Bennett, and carried on by [Lilburn W.] Boggs, [Thomas] Reynolds and [Thomas] Carlin, that superlative \uline{trio} of wickedness, abomination, and national disgrace. Sooner or later, however, corruption and wickedness get their reward.

					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-b}%
				Our
					\marginnote{b}%
				charters remain as they were, but that mobocratic influence of Missouri, which stained her broad prairies with innocent blood, crept into Illinois, and became visible in the house of Representatives of our state Legislature; but let corrupt men do what they may, ``wisdom will be justified of her children.''

					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-c}%
				You\sout{r}
					\marginnote{c}%
				have my grateful acknowledgements for your very kind interposition, in my behalf when ``Executive influence,'' was exerted to gratify and glut private malignance. It appears from <your> letter that Carlin paid no attention to your friendly admonition. I am not surprised at this, any more than the apostles were at the conduct of Nero at Rome.
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-d}%
				But
					\marginnote{d}%
				the smothered heat of a volcano, will generally burst forth and give sufficient time to escape its ``drea[d]ful lava''; and so, thank God, I am liberated, from his ``long reach'' by the still longer arm of United States Jurisprudence. Before the United States district court my case was fairly treated, and I was justly liberated:--- Always so in righteousness.

					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-e}%
				You
					\marginnote{e}%
				mention Bennett's book as a total failure: exactly so: No weapon formed against Zion can prosper: And he may learn, if [he] has not, that iniquity has its inconveniences, as well as greatness. Every one that has attempted to rise up and destroy me, or the church, has had the solemn opportunity and awful facts presented, that sudden and violent moves, too often bring shame and disgrace, in this world, and wretchedness and ruin, hereafter.
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-f}%
				No
					\marginnote{f}%
				government; no body of men, or person, can long exist, and do business in the sight of heaven without \uline{truth} and \uline{virtue}. You say, ``well, peace be to his manes,'' but permit me to declare, that while intelligence continues, \uline{there} \uline{will} \uline{be} \uline{no} \uline{peace} for the \uline{wicked}, according to scripture.

					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-g}%
				As
					\marginnote{g}%
				to James Gordon Bennett, I think he will be satisfied what my opinion is of him, when he reads the story of the ``lion'' in the Times and Seasons of Feb, 15 1843. I have no knowledge that he holds a commission in the Nauvoo Legion: His name is \sout{on} not on the Rank Roll. You can best account for this. Your relation of his intentions in extracting from the Times and Seasons, is all in course; the great object was too glaring upon the face of it to be mistaken, aside from the folly and vanity,--- and hotch potch which spotted his comments in the Herald.
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-h}%
				He
					\marginnote{h}%
				and all men should know that decency, virtue, honesty sobriety and truth, are among the most precious essentials of life, and especially in matters relating to eternity. There is a \uline{quid} \uline{pro} \uline{quo} in pure religion, by which \uline{pretentions} are weighed as well as actions and words; and so the promise is that God will bring every man into judgment, with every secret thing whether it be good or whether it be evil; and it is written in another place, that they shall be rewarded according to their works.

					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-i}%
				Your
					\marginnote{i}%
				goodly expression,--- ``Go ahead,'' \&c. will (as has been the case,) be fully verified, while God goes with me:--- And if you \sout{have a} <would only> \sout{notion} \sout{to} join me \sout{before you die}, \sout{count the cost as Jesus says,} and go for eternal reward in the celestial Kingdom of God, independent of mobs, persecutions, this world's goods, or the highest honors of the Mammon of unrighteousness. <Nothing could give me more joy.> 
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-j}%
				Were
					\marginnote{j}%
				I to labor for gold, or for conquest, or for empires like thousands that have flourished and fell, in all ages, \sout{of} before me, I might amass means; gather hosts; extend power, and gratify ambition, like an Alexander, or a Napoleon, and the world, in its ordinary course, would soon leave a wide waste for future conjecture and desecration:--- but I stand up in the midst of this crooked and perverse generation, for a general reformation of all; by the voice of truth; by the accomplishments of virtue;
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-17-MAR-1843-k}%
				by
					\marginnote{k}%
				the blessings of pure religion, and by the holy revelations of God, and thus far, over vexatious laws suits, mobs, ``spiritual wickedness in hi[g]h places,'' demons, devils and dev[ilish] men[,] in the name and by the power o[f] God, [\uline{I}] [\uline{a}]\uline{m} \uline{triumphant}: And while I have knowledge of heaven to guide me, and the riches of eternity to back me, I shall continue to strive for the emancipation of all kinds of slavery, as well as the slavery of sin; yea, until I can exclaim like Ca\"{e}sar: Veni, vidi, vici!

				\begin{tightright}
					Please to accept my best respects as also. t[h]ose of Mrs Smith, Dr [Willard] Richards and others. With considerations of the highest esteem, I have the honor to be your most obt. sev't.

					Joseph Smith

					<25>
				\end{tightright}

				<NAUVOO Ills. MAR 19>

				<(Single)>

				Maj. Gen. Jas. Arlington Bennet

				Arlington House

				New York

				N. Y.

				\textbf{Oh Lord my God thou that dwellest on high bless I beseach of thee the one into whose hands this may fall and crown her with a diadem of glory in the Eternal worlds Oh let <it> be Sealed this day on high that She Shall come forth in the first reserrection to recieve the Same and verily it Shall be so Saith the Lord if She remain in the Everlasting covenant to the end as also all her Fathers house Shall be Saved in the Same Eternal glory}

				\begin{tightright}
					\textbf{Joseph Smith}
				\end{tightright}
				
				\textbf{Sarah Ann Whitney)}

				Brother
					\marginnote{a}%
				Joseph [Kingsbury] I Lay my hands upon thy head in the name of Jesus Christ to bestow upon the[e] a Patriarkle Blessing according to the Power and authority of the Holy Priesthood vested in me I Say unto thee thou Shalt Be Blessed with <the> Good things of this wourld abundently in thy Lifetime and I Seal the[e] up to come forth in the first reserection unto Eternal Life--- 
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-23-MAR-1843-JK-b}%
				And
					\marginnote{b}%
				thy Companion Caroline [Whitney Kingsbury] who is now dead thou Shalt have in the first Reserection for I Seal thee up for and in her behalf to Come forth in the first Reserection unto Eternal lives (and it Shall be as though She was present herself) and thou Shalt hail her and She Shall be thine and no one Shall have power to take her from thee, And you both Shall be crowned and enthrowned to dwell together in a Kingdom in the Celestial Glory in the presents of God,
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-23-MAR-1843-JK-c}%
				And
					\marginnote{c}%
				you Shall Enjoy each other Society \& Embraces in all the fulness of the Gospell of Jesus Christ wourls [worlds] without End And I Seal these blessings upon thee and for thy Companion in the Name of Jesus Christ for thou Shalt receive the holy anointing \& Endewment in this Life to prepare you for all these blessings Even So Amen

		\subsubsection{Proclamation, 25 March 1843}
		% -----
			\label{EMS-1843-JS-25-MAR-1843}
			% \label{EMS-1843-JS-DAY-3LETTERMONTH-YEAR}
			
			\begin{description}

			\end{description}

				\begin{tabular}{p{0.5in}p{1in}p{0.5in}p{1in}}

				DC	 	& ---		& HC 		& --- \\
				HDDC 	& ---		& TCHC 		& --- \\
				\end{tabular}
				% HDDC = woodford's DC diss; JSR = Marquardt's JS Revelations; TCHC = Vogel HC
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Online Access] \href{https://www.josephsmithpapers.org/paper-summary/proclamation-25-march-1843/1}{Here}
				% \item[Analysis] \hyperref[XX]{Here}
			\end{description}
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Background]
			\end{description}
			
				% It might also be useful to give a two sentence context of the period: e.g., "This speech was given in Nauvoo to a small congregation one month prior to the destruction of the Nauvoo Expositor. These and other tensions may have been on the prophet's mind when he gave the speech."
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Original Source]
			\end{description}
			
				INTERIM CONTENT
			
				% brief description of original source material, with reference to JSP or somewhere else for more info
				% Background material: it might be helpful to have a note that says whether a given document was presented as a speech, was written in a journal, etc.
				% Also a comment here about how reliable the source might be, or what shape it is in, if there are large omissions or parts that are hard to understand, and so on.
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Text]
			\end{description}
			
				\begin{tightcenter}
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-25-MAR-1843-a}%
					PROCLAMATION,
						\marginnote{a}%
				\end{tightcenter}

				\textit{To the Citizens of Nauvoo.}

				Whereas it appears by the re-publication of the foregoing proceedings and declaration, that I have not altered my views on the subject of stealing: And whereas it is reported that there now exists a band of desperadoes, bound by oaths of secrecy, under severe penalties in case any member of the combination divulges their plans of stealing and conveying properties from station to station, up and down the Mississippi and other routes: And whereas it is reported that the fear of the execution of the pains and penalties of their secret oath, on their persons, prevents some of the members of said secret association, (who have, through falsehood and deceit, been drawn into their snares,) from divulging the same to the legally Constituted authorities of the land:

					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-25-MAR-1843-b}%
				Know
					\marginnote{b}%
				ye therefore, That I, Joseph Smith, Mayor of the city of Nauvoo, will grant and ensure protection against all personal mob violence, to each and every citizen of this city, who will freely and voluntarily come before me, and truly make known the names of all such abominable characters as are engaged in said secret combination for stealing, or are accessary thereto in any manner; and I would respectfully solicit the co-operation of all ministers of Justice, in this and the neighboring states, to ferret out a band of thievish oulaws from our midst.

				Given under my hand at Nauvoo City, this, 25th day of March, A. D., 1843.

				\begin{tightright}
					JOSEPH SMITH.

					Mayor of the said City.
				\end{tightright}
				
		% -----
		\subsubsection{Letter to Sidney Rigdon, 27 March 1843}
		% -----
			\label{EMS-1843-JS-27-MAR-1843}
			% \label{EMS-1843-JS-DAY-3LETTERMONTH-YEAR}
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Print Sources]
			\end{description}

				\begin{tabular}{p{0.5in}p{1in}p{0.5in}p{1in}}
				JSP	 	& ---		& JSR 		& --- \\
				DC	 	& ---		& HC 		& --- \\
				HDDC 	& ---		& TCHC 		& --- \\
				\end{tabular}
				% HDDC = woodford's DC diss; JSR = Marquardt's JS Revelations; TCHC = Vogel HC
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Online Access] \href{https://www.josephsmithpapers.org/paper-summary/letter-to-sidney-rigdon-27-march-1843/1}{Here}
				% \item[Analysis] \hyperref[XX]{Here}
			\end{description}
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Background]
			\end{description}
			
				% It might also be useful to give a two sentence context of the period: e.g., "This speech was given in Nauvoo to a small congregation one month prior to the destruction of the Nauvoo Expositor. These and other tensions may have been on the prophet's mind when he gave the speech."
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Original Source]
			\end{description}
			
				INTERIM CONTENT
			
				% brief description of original source material, with reference to JSP or somewhere else for more info
				% Background material: it might be helpful to have a note that says whether a given document was presented as a speech, was written in a journal, etc.
				% Also a comment here about how reliable the source might be, or what shape it is in, if there are large omissions or parts that are hard to understand, and so on.
			
			\begin{description}
				\item[Text]
			\end{description}
			
				\begin{tightright}
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-27-MAR-1843-a}%
					Nauvoo
						\marginnote{a}%
					March 27, 1843
				\end{tightright}

				Sidney Rigdon Esq\supers{r.}

				Dear Sir,

				It is with sensations of deep regret and poignant grief that I sit down to dictate a few lines to you, this morning, to let you know what my feelings \sout{in} are in relation to your-self, as it is against my principles to act the part of a hypocrite, or to dissemble in any wise whatever, with any man. I have tried for a long time to smother my feelings, and not let you know, that I thought, that you were secretly and underhandedly, doing all you could, to take the advantage and injure me: but, whether my feelings are right or wrong, remains for Eternity to reveal.
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-27-MAR-1843-b}%
				I
					\marginnote{b}%
				cannot any longer forbear throwing of[f] the mask, and let you know of the secret wranglings of my heart; that you may not be deceivd, in relation to them, and <that you may> be prepared, Sir, to take whatever cou[r]se you see proper in the premises. I am, Sir, honest, when I say that I believe, I <am> laboring under the fullest conviction that you are actually practicing deception and wickedness again[s]t me, and the chu[r]ch of Jesus Chri[s]t of Latter Day Saints.
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-27-MAR-1843-c}%
				and
					\marginnote{c}%
				that you are in connection with John C. Bennett, \& Geo W. Robinson, in the whole of their abominble practices in seeking to destroy me and this people and that Jared Carter, is as deep\sout{, Sir,} in the \sout{mud} mire, as you<, Sir,> are in the \sout{mud} mire, in your conspiracies, and that you are in the exerciese of a Traitrous spirit aga[ins]t our lives and interest by combining with our Enemies and the murderous Missourians, \uline{my} feelings, Sir, have been wrought upon to a very great extent, in relation to yourself, ever since soon after the first appearance of John C. Bennet in this place, there has been somethig dark \& my[s]terious hovering over our business concerns that are not only palpablle but altogether unacco[u]ntable in relation to the Post office,
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-27-MAR-1843-d}%
				and
					\marginnote{d}%
				Sir from the very first of the pretentions of John C. Bennet, to secure to me the Post Office, (which, by the by I have <never> desired, if I could have justice done me in that department,) <without my occupancy> I have known, Sir, that it was a fraud practiced upon me, and of the secret plottings \& conniving between, him \& yourself in relation to the matter the whole time, as well as many other things which I have kept locked up in my own bosom but I am constrained at this time, to make known my feelings to you.
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-27-MAR-1843-e}%
				I
					\marginnote{e}%
				do not write this with the intention of insulting you or of bearing down upon you or with a desire to take any advantage of you or with the intention of ever laying one straw in your way, detrimental to your character or influence, or to suffer any thing whatever that has taken place, which is within my observati[o]n, or that <has> come to my knowleidge to go abroad, betraying any confidence that has ever been placed in me but I do assure you most sincerly that what I have said I verily beleive.
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-27-MAR-1843-f}%
				\phantom{ }\& this
					\marginnote{f}%
				is the reason why I have said it, that you may know the real convictions of my heart. not be[c]ause I have any malice or hatred, neither would I injure one hair of your head, and I will assure you that these convictions are attended with the deepest sorrow \sout{\& remorse}. I wish to God it were not so, \& that I could get rid of the achings of my heart on that subject, and I now notify you, that unless something should take place to restore my mind to its former confidence in you, by some acknowledgmets on your part.
					\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-27-MAR-1843-g}%
				or
					\marginnote{g}%
				some explanations, that shall do away my Jealousies, I must as a conscientius man publish my withdrawal of \sout{your} my fellowship from you, to the Church, thr[o]ugh the medium of the times \& Seasons, and demand of the confernce a hearing concerning your case; that \sout{if}, on conviction of Justifiable grou[n]ds, they will demand your license. I could say much more but let the above suffice for the pre[se]nt,

				\begin{tightcenter}
					yours in haste,
				\end{tightcenter}

				\begin{tightright}
					Joseph Smith.---
				\end{tightright}

				Sidney Rigdon Esq\supers{r.}

				Post Office

				Nauvoo

		\subsubsection{Instruction, 2 April 1843}
		% -----
			\label{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843}
			% \label{EMS-1843-JS-DAY-3LETTERMONTH-YEAR}
			
			% --
			\paragraph{As Reported by William Clayton}
			% --
				\label{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC}
			
				\begin{description}
					\item[Print Sources]
				\end{description}

					\begin{tabular}{p{0.5in}p{1in}p{0.5in}p{1in}}
					JSP	 	& ---		& JSR 		& --- \\
					DC	 	& ---		& HC 		& --- \\
					HDDC 	& ---		& TCHC 		& --- \\
					\end{tabular}
					% HDDC = woodford's DC diss; JSR = Marquardt's JS Revelations; TCHC = Vogel HC
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Online Access] \href{https://www.josephsmithpapers.org/paper-summary/instruction-2-april-1843-as-reported-by-william-clayton/1}{Here}
					% \item[Analysis] \hyperref[XX]{Here}
				\end{description}
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Background]
				\end{description}
				
					% It might also be useful to give a two sentence context of the period: e.g., "This speech was given in Nauvoo to a small congregation one month prior to the destruction of the Nauvoo Expositor. These and other tensions may have been on the prophet's mind when he gave the speech."
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Original Source]
				\end{description}
				
					INTERIM CONTENT
				
					% brief description of original source material, with reference to JSP or somewhere else for more info
					% Background material: it might be helpful to have a note that says whether a given document was presented as a speech, was written in a journal, etc.
					% Also a comment here about how reliable the source might be, or what shape it is in, if there are large omissions or parts that are hard to understand, and so on.
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Text]
				\end{description}
				
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-a}%
					P. M.
						\marginnote{a}%
					Joseph preached on Revelations chap. 5. he called on me to open the meeting. He also preachd on the same subject in the evening. During the day president Joseph made the following remarks on doctrine.

					\begin{quote}
							\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-b}%
						I
							\marginnote{b}%
						was once praying very earnestly to know \sout{to} the time of the comeing of the son of man when I heard a voice repeat the following `Joseph my son, if thou livest untill thou art 85 years old thou shalt see the face of the son of man, therefore let this suffice and trouble me no more on this matter.'

							\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-c}%
						I
							\marginnote{c}%
						was left \sout{this} thus without being able to decide wether this coming referred to the beginning of the Millenium, or to some previous appearing, or wether I should die and thus see his face. I believe the coming of the son of man will not be any sooner than that time
					\end{quote}

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-d}%
					In
						\marginnote{d}%
					correct<ing> two points in E[lde]r Hydes [Orson Hyde's] discourse he observed as follows,

					\begin{quote}
						The meaning of that passage where it reads `when he shall appear we shall be like him for we shall see him as he is' is this, When the saviour appears we shall see that he is a man like unto ourselves, and that same \hyperref[SOCIALITY]{sociality} which exists amongst us here will exist among us there only it will be coupled with eternal glory which we do not enjoy now.
						
							\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-e}%
						Also
							\marginnote{e}%
						The appearing of the father and the son in \hyperref[JOHN-14-23]{John c 14 v 23} is a personal appearing, and the idea that they will dwell in a mans heart is a sectarian doctrine and is false
					\end{quote}					

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-f}%
					In
						\marginnote{f}%
					answer to a question which I proposed to him as follows, `Is not the reckoning of Gods time, Angels time, prophets time \& mans time according to the planet on which they reside he answered Yes

					\begin{quote}
							\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-g}%
						But
							\marginnote{g}%
						there is no Angel ministers to this earth only what either does belong or has belonged to this earth And the Angels do not reside on a planet like our earth but they dwell with God and the planet where he dwells is like crystal, and like a sea of glass before the throne. This is the great Urim \& Thummim whereon all things are manifest both things past, present \& future and are continually before the Lord.
							\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-h}%
						The
							\marginnote{h}%
						Urim \& Thummim is a small representation of this globe. The earth when it is purified will be made like unto crystal and will be a Urim \& Thummim whereby all things pertaining to an inferior kingdom, or all kingdoms of a lower order will be manifest to those who dwell on it.
							\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-i}%
						And
							\marginnote{i}%
						this earth will be with Christ Then the white stone mentioned in \hyperref[REVELATIONNT-2-17]{Rev. c 2 v 17} is the Urim \& Thummim whereby all things pertaining to an higher order of kingdoms even all kingdoms will be made known and \sout{a} the white stone is given to each of those who come into \sout{this} the celestial kingdom, whereon is a new name written which no man knoweth save he that receiveth it. The new name is the key word.

							\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-j}%
						Whatever
							\marginnote{j}%
						\hyperref[PRINCIPAL]{principal} of intelligence we obtain in this life will rise with us in the resurrection: and if a person gains more knowledge in this life through his diligence \& obedience than another, he will have so much the advantage in the world to come.
							\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-k}%
						There
							\marginnote{k}%
						is a law irrevocably decreed in heaven before the foundations of this world upon which all blessings are predicated; and when we obtain any blessing from God, it is by obedience to that law upon which it is predicated.

							\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-l}%
						The
							\marginnote{l}%
						Holy Ghost is a personage, and a person cannot have the personage of the H. G. in his heart. A man may receive the gifts of the H. G, and the H. G. may descend upon a man but not to tarry with him.
					\end{quote}

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-m}%
					He
						\marginnote{m}%
					also related the following dream

					\begin{quote}
						I dreamed that a silver-headed old man came to me and said he was invaded by a gang of robbers, who were plundering his neighbors and threatening destruction to all his subjects. He had heard that I always sought to defend the oppressed, and he had come to see if the General would call out his Legion and protect him, and he had come to hear with his own ears what answer I would give him.
							\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-n}%
						I
							\marginnote{n}%
						answered, if you will make out the papers and shew that you are not the aggressor I will call out the Legion and defend you while I have a man to stand by me. The old man then turned to go away. When he got a little distance he turned suddenly round and said I must call out the Legion and go and he would have the papers ready when I arrived, and says he I have any amount of men which you can have under your command.
					\end{quote}

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-o}%
					Er
						\marginnote{o}%
					Hyde gave \sout{the} this interpretation

					\begin{quote}
						The old man represents the government of these United States who will be invaded by a foreign foe, probably England. The U. S. gorvernment will call on you to defend probably all this Western Territory, and will offer you any amount of men you may need for that purpose.
					\end{quote}

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-p}%
					Once
						\marginnote{p}%
					when pres\supersu{t}\supers{.} Joseph was praying earnestly to know concerning the wars which are to preceed the coming of the son of man, he heard a voice proclaim that the first outbreak of general bloodshed would commence at South Carolina--- see Revelation

					The sealing of the 144000 was the number of priests who should be anointed to administer in the daily sacrifice \&c.

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WC-q}%
					During
						\marginnote{q}%
					pres\supersu{t}\supers{.} Joseph's remarks he said their was a nice distinction between the vision which John saw as spoken of in Revelations \& the vision which Daniel saw. the former relating \sout{only} to things as they actually existed in heaven--- the latter being a figure representing things on the earth. God never made use of the figure of a beast to represent the kingdom of heaven--- when they were made use of it was to represent an apostate church.
					
			% --
			\paragraph{As Reported by Willard Richards}
			% --
				\label{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR}
			
				\begin{description}
					\item[Print Sources]
				\end{description}

					\begin{tabular}{p{0.5in}p{1in}p{0.5in}p{1in}}
					JSP	 	& ---		& JSR 		& --- \\
					DC	 	& ---		& HC 		& --- \\
					HDDC 	& ---		& TCHC 		& --- \\
					\end{tabular}
					% HDDC = woodford's DC diss; JSR = Marquardt's JS Revelations; TCHC = Vogel HC
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Online Access] \href{https://www.josephsmithpapers.org/paper-summary/instruction-2-april-1843-as-reported-by-willard-richards/1}{Here}
					% \item[Analysis] \hyperref[XX]{Here}
				\end{description}
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Background]
				\end{description}
				
					% It might also be useful to give a two sentence context of the period: e.g., "This speech was given in Nauvoo to a small congregation one month prior to the destruction of the Nauvoo Expositor. These and other tensions may have been on the prophet's mind when he gave the speech."
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Original Source]
				\end{description}
				
					INTERIM CONTENT
				
					% brief description of original source material, with reference to JSP or somewhere else for more info
					% Background material: it might be helpful to have a note that says whether a given document was presented as a speech, was written in a journal, etc.
					% Also a comment here about how reliable the source might be, or what shape it is in, if there are large omissions or parts that are hard to understand, and so on.
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Text]
				\end{description}
				
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-a}%
					Elder
						\marginnote{a}%
					\phantom{ }[Orson] Hyde I am going to offer some corrections to you. Elder H. repli[e]d--- they shall be thankfully recieved.--- When he shall appear we shall see him as he is. we shall see that he is a man like ourselves.--- And that same sociality. which exists amogt [amongst] us here. will exist amo[n]g us there only it will be coupled with eternl glory which glory we do not now enjoy.

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-b}%
					14
						\marginnote{b}%
					John 23.--- the appearing of the father and of the Son in that verse is a personal. appearance.--- to say that the father and the Son dwells in a mans heart is an old Sectarian notion. and is not correct.

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-c}%
					There
						\marginnote{c}%
					\sout{is} are no angels who administer to this earth but who belong \sout{to} or have belongd to this earth. The angels do not reside on a planet like this earth. but they reside in the presence of God--- but on a Globe like a sea of glass. \& fire. ``sea of glass before the throne.--- \&c.'' where all things are manif[e]st past present \& to come,---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-d}%
					The
						\marginnote{d}%
					place where God resides is a great Urim And Thumim.

					This earth in its sanctifid \& immortal state. will be a Urim \& Thummim for all things below it in the scale of creation. but not above it.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-e}%
					related
						\marginnote{e}%
					the Dream. written on page 3\supersu{d} Book <B>

					Interpretati[o]n By O. Hyde--- old man.--- Govermnt of these Unitd States, who will be invaded by a foriegn foe. probably England. U. S. Goverment will call on Gen Smith to defend probably all this western territory and offer him any am[ou]nt of men he shall desire \& put them under his command.

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-f}%
					I
						\marginnote{f}%
					prophecy in the Name of the Lord God that the commenceme[n]t of bloodshed as preparat[o]ry to the coming of the son of man. will commenc[e] in South Carolina.--- (it probably may arise through the slave trade.)--- this \sout{the} a voice declard to me. while I was praying earne[s]tly on the subje[c]t 25 December 1832.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-g}%
					I
						\marginnote{g}%
					earnestly desird to know concern[in]g the coming of the Son of Man \& prayed. when--- a voice said to me, Joseph, my, son, if thou livest until thou art 85 years old thou shalt see the facce of the son of man. therefore let this suffice \& trouble me no more on this matter.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-h}%
					1.
						\marginnote{h}%
					P. M. attended meeting.

					Joseph read 5.\supersu{th} chapter of \sout{Johns} revelation, referring particularly to the 6[\supersu{th}] verse. shewing from that. the a[c]tual existenc[e] of beasts in heaven probable those were beasts which had lived on another planet. than our's---

					God never made use of the figure of a beast to represent the kingdom of heaven.--- Beasts. 7 eyes Pr[i]esthood.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-i}%
					this
						\marginnote{i}%
					is the fi[r]st time I have ever taken a text in Revelati[o]n.--- and if the young elders would let such things alone it would be far better.--- then corrected Elder Hyde as in private.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-j}%
					supped
						\marginnote{j}%
					at Bro [Benjamin F.] Johnson's.--- expected to sta[r]t for Carthage, but bad weather prevntd [prevented] called another meeti[n]g. by bell.--- read Rev[elation]--- between Meeti[n]gs with Elder Hyde \& expou[n]ding.--- during this time several came in \& expressd fear that I had come in contact with the old scripture

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-k}%
					Meeting
						\marginnote{k}%
					7. eve resumd the subjct of the beast.--- shewed very plainly that Johns vision was very different from Daniels Prophecy--- one refering to things ex[is]ting in heaven. the other a figure of things \sout{on the} which are on the earth.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-l}%
					whatever
						\marginnote{l}%
					principle of inteligence we attain unto in this life. it will rise with us in the revalatin [revelation], and if a person gains more knowledge and intelignce. through his obedience \& diligence. than another he will have so much the advantage in the world to come---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-m}%
					There
						\marginnote{m}%
					is a law irrevocably decreed in heaven. before the foundation of the world upon which all blessings are predicated and when we obtain a blessing it is by obedi[e]nce to the law upon which that blessing is predicated.

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-n}%
					again
						\marginnote{n}%
					revertd to Elders Hyde mistake. \&c the Father has a body of flesh \& bones as tangible as mans the Son also, but the Holy Ghost is a personage of spirit.--- and a person cannot have the personage <of the H G. [Holy Ghost]> in his heart he may recive the gift of the holy Ghost. it may descend upon him but not to tarry with him.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-o}%
					What
						\marginnote{o}%
					is the meaning of the scriptures. he that is faithful over a few thi[n]gs shall be made ruler over many? \& he that is faithful over many shall be made ruler over many more?

					What is the mea[n]ing of the Parable of the 10 talents? [\textit{blank}] Also [\textit{blank}] conversation with Nicodemus. except a man be born of water \& of the spi[ri]t.---

					I shall not tell you?---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-2-APR-1843-WR-p}%
					Closed
						\marginnote{p}%
					by flagellating the audience for their fears.--- \& calld upon Elder Hyde to get up. \& fulfil his covenant to preach 1/4 of an hour.--- otherwise I will give you a good whipping.---

					Elder Hyde arose \& said Brothe[r]s \& Sisters I feel as though all had been Said that can be said. I can say nothing but bless you.---

					To B. F. Johnsons------ \sout{one} the 144000 seald are the pri[e]sts who are appointed to administer in the daily sacrifice.--- [\textit{6 lines blank}]
					
		% -----
		\subsubsection{Minutes and Discourses, 6--8 April 1843}
		% -----
			\label{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843}
			% \label{EMS-1843-JS-DAY-3LETTERMONTH-YEAR}
			
			% --
			\paragraph{As Reported by Willard Richards}
			% --
				\label{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR}
			
				\begin{description}
					\item[Print Sources]
				\end{description}

					\begin{tabular}{p{0.5in}p{1in}p{0.5in}p{1in}}
					JSP	 	& ---		& JSR 		& --- \\
					DC	 	& ---		& HC 		& --- \\
					HDDC 	& ---		& TCHC 		& --- \\
					\end{tabular}
					% HDDC = woodford's DC diss; JSR = Marquardt's JS Revelations; TCHC = Vogel HC
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Online Access] \href{https://www.josephsmithpapers.org/paper-summary/minutes-and-discourses-6-8-april-1843/1}{Here}
					% \item[Analysis] \hyperref[XX]{Here}
				\end{description}
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Background]
				\end{description}
				
					% It might also be useful to give a two sentence context of the period: e.g., "This speech was given in Nauvoo to a small congregation one month prior to the destruction of the Nauvoo Expositor. These and other tensions may have been on the prophet's mind when he gave the speech."
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Original Source]
				\end{description}
				
					INTERIM CONTENT
					
					Careful with this one too, it's sometimes hard to tell who is speaking in these notes.
					
					Note also that this account here by Willard Richards contains both the ``A'' discourse and ``B'' discourse on the JSP website.
				
					% brief description of original source material, with reference to JSP or somewhere else for more info
					% Background material: it might be helpful to have a note that says whether a given document was presented as a speech, was written in a journal, etc.
					% Also a comment here about how reliable the source might be, or what shape it is in, if there are large omissions or parts that are hard to understand, and so on.
				
				\begin{description}
					\item[Text]
				\end{description}
					
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-a}%
					...
						\marginnote{a}%
					O. Pratt gave way \& Joseph. rose to state the object of the meeting. It is my object to ascertain the stanig [standing] of the first presidency. (as I have been instructed) I present myself for trial, I shall next present my councillors for trial.--- 3\supersu{d} to take into consideration the sending out of the twelve <or some porti[o]n of them> or some body else to get means to build \sout{the} up Nauvoo. House--- \sout{\& temple}
					
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-b}%
					4---
						\marginnote{b}%
					Elders will have the privelige of appeals from the differce conferences to this if there are any such cases.--- It is important that this conference give importance to the N[auvoo] House. as a prejudice exists against the Nauvoo. House in favor of the Lords House------

					There is no place where men of wealth \& character \& influence can go to repose thimselves. and it is neccessary we should have such a place.

					Are you satisfied with the first presedincy, so far as I am concerned, or will you choose another? If I have done any thing to injure my character in the sight of men \& angels--- or men \& women. come forward tell of it. \& if not ever after hold your peace.

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-c}%
					President
						\marginnote{c}%
					B. Young arose \& nominatd Joseph Smith to contin[u]e as \sout{our} the Presdent. <of the church> Orson Hyde 2\supersu{dd} it.--- Voted unanimously--- Such a show of hands was never seen before in the church.--- Joseph retur[ne]d his thanks------ to the assembly. \& said he would serve them according to the best of his ability...

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-d}%
					\phantom{ }<Joseph
						\marginnote{d}%
					said I do not know any thing agai[n]st the twelve, if I did I would presnt them for trial.>

					It is not right that all the burden of the Nauvoo House should rest on a few individuals---

					and <we> will now consider the prepriety of sending the twelve to collect means for the Nauvoo House.---

					there has been too great latitude in individuals for the building of the Temple to the exclusion of the Nauvoo house.

					It. has been reportd that the------ twelve have wages \$2.00 per day for \sout{their services} <I never heard--- this till recently. \& I do not believe I have never known their having any thing> I go in for binding up the twelve, \sout{\&}

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-e}%
					Let
						\marginnote{e}%
					this confirence institute an order to this end <\&> let no man pay money or stock into the hands of the twelve\sout{s} except the \sout{payee} payer transmit the account immediately to the Trustee'\sout{s} in trust. \& no man else. <but the twelve> have authority to act as agents for the Temple \& Nauvoo House.------

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-f}%
					I
						\marginnote{f}%
					will mention one case--- he is a good man. that's man's name is Russel [Samuel M. Russell]. he had been East on bussines[s] for his brothe[r]. \& took money belonging to the temple. \& put it in the bag with his brothers money. 2 or three days after <his return> he called on his brother, for the money. but his brother thought he had paid out too much money--- \& he would keep the chu[r]ch money.--- <to make good his own> I called to see Russel [Daniel Russell] about the money. and he treated me so politely I concluded he never meant to pay--- Bro Russel [Samuel Russell]. said, <that> his brother said he should not be out of money again.--- There was \$20. of the chu[r]ch money, \& some dried apple for the Prest.

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-g}%
					I
						\marginnote{g}%
					propose that \sout{your} you send moneys for the temple by the twelve some or all; or some agent of your choosing \& if you send by others \& the money is lost, tis lost to yourselves. I cannot be responsible for it.

					It is wrong for the church to make a bridge of my nose in appropriati[n]g chu[r]ch funds.------ The incorporation requ[ire]d of me securietis [securities].--- which were lodged in the proper hands---

					Temple committee \sout{have} are bound to me in <the sum> \$2,000--- \& the chu[r]ch is running to them <with funds> every day--- \& I am not responsible for it.

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-h}%
					so
						\marginnote{h}%
					long as you consider me worthy to hold this office. it is your duty to attend to the legal forms belonging--- to the business.--- My desire is, <that> the conference minutes go forth, to inform all bra[n]ches of the order of doing business. \& the twelve be appointd to this spicial mission of collecting funds for the Nauvoo House.---

					when I went to the white House at Washigtn [Washington]. <\&> presented Letters from Thomas Carlin. [Martin] Van Buren. said <Tho\supersu{s} Carlin. Thos Carlin> whos Thos Carlin?--- I erred in spirit,--- \& <I> confess my mistake, in being angry with Martin Van Buren for saying Tho\supersu{s} Carlin is nobody.--- let it be recorded on earth and in heaven that I am clear of this sin.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-i}%
					There
						\marginnote{i}%
					has been complaints again[s]t the Temple Committee.--- for appropriati[n]g the chu[r]ch funds to the benefit of their own children. to the neglect of others who need assistanc[e] more than they do.---

					I have <the> complaint, by W\supersu{m} Clayton. W\supersu{m}\supers{.} Clayton called. Says I have to say to the confernce. I am not so fully prepreded [prepared] to \sout{present} substantiate the proof as I could wish---

					I am able to prove that partiality used to a great extent. I am able to prove by the books that [Reynolds] Cahoon \& Higby [Elias Higbee] have used property for their own families--- to the exclusion of others.------ <Joseph said> Let the. trial of the Committee be deferrd to another day--- <then let> the Lion \& unicorn--- come together--- day after tomorrow. Mr Clayton can have the privelige of bringing his books. to the trial.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-j}%
					\phantom{ }
						\marginnote{j}%
					\phantom{ }\sout{Voted} \sout{that} \sout{the} Moved and Seconded \sout{\&} voted that the twelve be appointed a committe[e] to receive \& gather funds to build the Nauvoo House. [\textit{blank}] with this proviso, that the twelve \sout{and} give bonds \sout{to} for good delivery. to trustee in trustee.--- \& \sout{payee} payer make immediate report to the trustee in trust.--- Bro W[illiam] W. Phelps proposd that the twelve give duplicate receipt's.

					President Young. remarked he should never give reciepts for cash. <except such as he put in his own pocket for his own use.> but wished this speculation to stop.--- \& asked \sout{when} if any one knew any thing against any one of the twelve, any dishonesty. I know of one who is not.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-k}%
					\phantom{ }
						\marginnote{k}%
					\phantom{ }\sout{Joseph} <And referred to muzzling the ox that treadetheth out the corn.--->

					Joseph Said, I will answer Bro Brigham,--- let the twelve spend the time belonging to the temple for to collect funds--- and the remainder of the time they may labor for their support.---

					The idea of not muzzling the ox that treadeth out the corn--- is a good old quaker song.--- I have never taken the first farthing of church funds for my own use. till I have first consulted the proper author[i]ties.--- \& when there was no quorum of the twelve or high priests I have asked the temple Committee who had \sout{not} no business with it.--- Elder [Alpheus] Cutler said it was so.--- Let this conference stop all agents in collecting funds, except the twelve.------...

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-l}%
					Joseph
						\marginnote{l}%
					followed. I want the Elders to make hono[ra]ble proclamati[o]n abroad what the feelings of the presidency are,--- I despise a theif above ground. He would betray me <if he could get the oppetinity [opportunity]> if I were the biggest rogue in the world, he would steal my horse when I wanted to run away. <then> read prloclamatin [proclamation] of the Mayor on stealing. dated 25. day March 1843.--- ``Wasp.'' No. 48[\supersu{th}]--- many observati[o]n confirmatory \& said, enough Said. for this conference on this Subject.---

					Elders \sout{had} have a privilige to appeal from any decision of a branch. to know if they shall retain their office or membership---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-m}%
					necessary
						\marginnote{m}%
					I explain concerni[n]g Keokuk it is known that the Govr of Iowa has granted a writ. for me. on affidav[i]t of [Lilburn W.] Boggs.--- he still holds that writ as a cudgel over my head. (U. S. Atttorney told me all writs issued thus were legally dead.)--- I said that is a stumper \& I will shew them a trick the Devil never did. <that is> leave them. every man who wishes to out economically. with regard to futurity.--- let them come over here as soon as they can settle their affairs without sacrifice let them come \& we will protect them \& let that governme[n]t know that we don't like to be imposed upon.---

						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-n}%
					about
						\marginnote{n}%
					the first of August 1842 Mr [Jacob] Remick came to my house. put on a long face. said he was in distress. about to loose \$1400 for <a debt of> 300 at sheriffs sale. <said he the> sale takes place to-morrow.--- I have money in St Louis . . .--- next morning he called. I did not like the looks of him. <but thought I> he is a stranger. I have been a stranger, \&. better loose 200 than be guilty of sin of ingratitude. took his note. on demand. <the> day I was taken I asked him from [for?] the money.--- you ought to have it <said he but> I have not got my mony from St Louis.--- I have a curious plan in my mind.
						\hypertarget{EMS-1843-JS-6-8-APR-1843-WR-o}%
					I
						\marginnote{o}%
